We obtain similar results with missing values and discretization: point estimates remain stable and the confidence intervals adaptively increase in length for higher noise-to-signal ratios, similar to Figure
5
. Appendix
L
confirms similar results when standardizing the true covariates before the semi-synthetic exercises.

(a)

Measurement error

(b)

Missing values

(c)

Discretization

(d)

Differential privacy (calibrated)

Figure 6
:
Synthetic corruption

Formalizing privacy
. Next, we calibrate our semi-synthetic exercise to privacy levels mandated by the US Census Bureau. To do so, we clarify how our model of causal inference with corrupted data accommodates differential privacy mechanisms. With these formal results, we calibrate the variance of the Laplacian noise appropriately. In what follows, we focus on a one-off data release (formally called the non-interactive setting).

We maintain the following thought experiment: we are the Census Bureau, and our goal is to publish
[
Autor et al., 2013
]
’s CZ-level aggregated data set while protecting the privacy of individuals within CZs. In particular, we have access to the individual-level microdata, which we will
not
publicly share; we will only publish the CZ-level summaries for aggregate units. Consider a particular commuting zone
i\in[n]
with
L_{i}
individuals, and denote its individual-level microdata by
\boldsymbol{M}^{(i)}\in\mathbb{R}^{L_{i}\times p}
. We wish to publish
p
summary statistics
X_{i,\cdot}
for this CZ, where
X_{ij}=\frac{1}{L_{i}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L_{i}}M^{(i)}_{\ell j}
, however we wish to maintain plausible deniability that each individual
\ell\in[L_{i}]
contributed their data. The simulated attack on the 2010 Census found that Census block summary tables did not maintain this plausible deniability.

Definition 6.1

(Differential privacy for summary tables)
.

A randomized mechanism
\mathcal{M}
confers differential privacy with privacy loss
\epsilon
if and only if for any two possible individual-level data sets
\boldsymbol{M}
and
\boldsymbol{M}^{\prime}
differing in a single row, and for all events
E
in the range of
\mathcal{M}
,

\frac{\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{M}(\boldsymbol{M})\in E)}{\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{M}(\boldsymbol{M}^{\prime})\in E)}\leq e^{\epsilon}

where the randomness is with respect to
\mathcal{M}
.

The canonical mechanism that achieves differential privacy is to publish
\mathcal{M}(\boldsymbol{M}^{(i)})=X_{i,\cdot}+H_{i,\cdot}
instead of
X_{i,\cdot}
, where
H_{i,\cdot}
is Laplacian noise with an appropriately calibrated variance.
7
7

7

More precisely,
\mathcal{M}_{i}:\mathbb{R}^{L_{i}\times p}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{p}
.

In addition to the Laplace mechanism, the
discrete Gaussian, piece wise uniform, and bounded mechanisms
induce measurement error that is subexponential and conditionally mean zero, which fits within our framework.
For simplicity, we focus on the Laplace mechanism when relating privacy to our theoretical results.
8
8

8

Bureau policies mandate “zero concentrated” differential privacy, which is a closely related privacy concept for which our implementation suffices. See Appendix
M
for details.

Proposition 6.1

(Strong protections for aggregate data)
.

Suppose (i) each entry of microdata is bounded, i.e.
|M^{(i)}_{\ell j}|\leq\bar{A}_{i}
;
(ii) no individual appears in two commuting zones.
Then the mechanism
Z_{ij}=X_{ij}+H_{ij}
where
H_{ij}\overset{i.i.d.}{\sim}\text{Laplace}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2\bar{A}_{i}}{\epsilon}\frac{p}{L_{i}}}}\right)
confers
\epsilon
differential privacy and the measurement error parameters satisfy

K_{a},\kappa\leq\max_{i\in[n]}\frac{2^{3/2}\bar{A}_{i}}{\epsilon}\frac{p}{L_{i}}.
This privacy guarantee is immune to data cleaning.

Corollary 6.1

(Safety in numbers)
.

Suppose the conditions of Proposition
6.1
hold and
\max_{i\in[n]}\frac{p}{L_{i}}\lesssim\ln(np)
. Then the measurement error parameters satisfy
K_{a},\kappa\lesssim\ln(np)
, and therefore our rates of data cleaning and error-in-variable estimation translate into data cleaning adjusted confidence intervals.

In summary, the calibrated Laplacian variance depends on the privacy loss
\epsilon
, the most extreme true value
\bar{A}_{i}
, the number of covariates
p
, and number of individuals
L_{i}
per aggregate unit.
The auxiliary condition
\frac{p}{L_{i}}\lesssim\ln(np)
is a practical diagnostic: roughly speaking, the number of published covariates should not greatly exceed the number of individuals per aggregate unit. It sheds light on limitations because it is plausible for CZs, but implausible for Census blocks. Much empirical economic research studies CZs, which we study in our semi-synthetic exercise. Future research may empirically investigate, through simulated attacks, how vulnerable various data releases may be for different
\frac{p}{L_{i}}
regimes.

Figure
6(d)
implements differential privacy for
[
Autor et al., 2013
]
’s CZ-level aggregated data set while protecting the privacy of individuals within CZs. We calibrate the Laplacian variance according to Proposition
6.1
, where
\epsilon
is based on Bureau memos,
p=30
in the augmented specification, and
(\bar{A}_{i},L_{i})
are calculated from the microdata for each CZ; see Appendix
M
for details.
To study the robustness of our results to the privacy loss parameter, we consider
(100\epsilon,10\epsilon,\epsilon,0.1\epsilon,0.01\epsilon)
, which corresponds to privacy below and above the mandated level. Across levels, our point estimates and confidence intervals remain stable.

7
Conclusion

Recent developments in how the US Census Bureau publishes economic data motivate us to study a class of corruptions that is rich enough to encompass classical types of corruption, such as measurement error and missingness, as well as modern types, such as discretization and differential privacy mechanisms. Abstractly, our goal is to learn parameters in nonlinear, heterogeneous causal models from corrupted data; concretely, our goal is to characterize some scenarios in which it is possible to achieve both privacy and precision. To do so, we propose new data cleaning-adjusted confidence intervals that are computationally simple, statistically rigorous, and empirically robust in settings calibrated to empirical economic research. We build a framework to use matrix completion as data cleaning for downstream causal inference, bridging two rich literatures. Future work may extend our results to confounded noise and sample selection bias.

Appendix

Appendix A
Data cleaning

In this appendix, we replace the symbol
X_{i,\cdot}
with the symbol
A_{i,\cdot}
.
We suppress indexing by the folds
(\textsc{train},\textsc{test})
to lighten notation. As in Assumption
5.3
, we identify
NA
with
0
in
\boldsymbol{Z}
hereafter. We slightly abuse notation by letting
n
be the number of observations in
train
, departing from the notation of the main text. The entire section is conditional on
\boldsymbol{A}
, which we omit. We write
\|\cdot\|=\|\cdot\|_{op}
and let
C
be an absolute constant.

Recall
\boldsymbol{A}=\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}+\boldsymbol{E}^{\textsc{(lr)}}
and
r=rank\{\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\}
. We denote the SVDs

\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}=\boldsymbol{U}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{V}^{T}
,
\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}=\hat{\boldsymbol{U}}_{k}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{k}\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k}^{T}
, and
\boldsymbol{Z}\bhrho^{-1}=\hat{\boldsymbol{U}}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}^{T}.

The first
k
left singular vectors of
\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}
are
\boldsymbol{U}_{k}
. We denote
s_{k}=\Sigma_{kk}
and
\hat{s}_{k}=\hat{\Sigma}_{kk}
.

Recall that
\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\textsc{train}}
is constructed by taking
train
covariates then filling and cleaning them using
train
alone. As a theoretical device we also study
\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\textsc{test}}
, obtained by taking
test
covariates, filling them using
train
, and cleaning them using
test
. The analysis does not depend on whether
\bhrho^{\textsc{train}}
or
\bhrho^{\textsc{test}}
is used when filling in missing values.

Consider a matrix
\boldsymbol{B}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times p}
with SVD
\boldsymbol{B}=\sum_{i=1}^{n\wedge p}\sigma_{i}u_{i}v_{i}^{T}
.
We define the linear function
\varphi^{\boldsymbol{B}}_{\lambda}:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
as

\varphi^{\boldsymbol{B}}_{\lambda}(w)=\sum_{i=1}^{n\wedge p}1(\sigma_{i}\geq\lambda)u_{i}u_{i}^{T}w.

We use the shorthand
\varphi^{\boldsymbol{B}}=\varphi_{0}^{\boldsymbol{B}}
.

Define the events:

\mathcal{E}_{1}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\|\boldsymbol{Z}-\boldsymbol{A}\boldsymbol{\rho}\|\leq(\sqrt{n}+\sqrt{p})\Delta_{H,op}}}\right\},\quad\mathcal{E}_{2}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\max_{j\in[p]}\|Z_{\cdot,j}-\rho_{j}A_{\cdot,j}\|_{2}^{2}\leq n\Delta_{H}}}\right\},

\mathcal{E}_{3}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\max_{j\in[p]}\|\boldsymbol{U}_{k}\boldsymbol{U}^{T}_{k}(Z_{\cdot,j}-\rho_{j}A_{\cdot,j})\|_{2}^{2}\leq k\Delta_{H}}}\right\},\quad\mathcal{E}_{4}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\forall j\in[p],\;\frac{1}{\delta}\rho_{j}\leq\hat{\rho}_{j}\leq\delta\rho_{j}}}\right\},

\mathcal{E}_{5}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\max_{j\in[p]}|\hat{\rho}_{j}-\rho_{j}|\leq C\sqrt{\frac{\ln(np)}{n}}}}\right\},

where

\Delta_{H,op}=C\bar{A}(\kappa+K_{a}+\bar{K})\ln^{\frac{3}{2}}(np),\quad\Delta_{H}=C(K_{a}+\bar{A}\bar{K})^{2}\ln^{2}(np),\quad\delta=\frac{1}{1-\sqrt{\frac{22\ln(np)}{n\rho_{\min}}}}.

The Online Appendix shows that under Assumptions
5.1
,
5.2
, and
5.3
,

\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}^{c})\leq\frac{10}{n^{10}p^{10}},

where
\mathcal{E}:=\cap_{k=1}^{5}\mathcal{E}_{k}
.

Lemma A.1
.

Set
k=r
. Then,

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{Z}\hat{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{-1}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|~\Big|~\{\mathcal{E}_{1},\mathcal{E}_{4},\mathcal{E}_{5}\}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\delta}{\rho_{\min}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((\sqrt{n}+\sqrt{p})\Delta_{H,op}+\|\boldsymbol{E}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|+\sqrt{\frac{\ln(np)}{n}}\|\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|}}\right);

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{U}\boldsymbol{U}^{T}-\hat{\boldsymbol{U}}_{r}\hat{\boldsymbol{U}}_{r}^{T}\|~\Big|~\{\mathcal{E}_{1},\mathcal{E}_{4},\mathcal{E}_{5}\}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\delta}{\rho_{\min}s_{r}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((\sqrt{n}+\sqrt{p})\Delta_{H,op}+\|\boldsymbol{E}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|+\sqrt{\frac{\ln(np)}{n}}\|\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|}}\right);

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{V}\boldsymbol{V}^{T}-\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{r}\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{r}^{T}\|~\Big|~\{\mathcal{E}_{1},\mathcal{E}_{4},\mathcal{E}_{5}\}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\delta}{\rho_{\min}s_{r}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{(\sqrt{n}+\sqrt{p})\Delta_{H,op}+\|\boldsymbol{E}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|+\sqrt{\frac{\ln(np)}{n}}\|\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|}}\right\};

\displaystyle|s_{r}-\hat{s}_{r}|~\Big|~\{\mathcal{E}_{1},\mathcal{E}_{4},\mathcal{E}_{5}\}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\delta}{\rho_{\min}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{(\sqrt{n}+\sqrt{p})\Delta_{H,op}+\|\boldsymbol{E}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|+\sqrt{\frac{\ln(np)}{n}}\|\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|}}\right\}.

Proof.

To begin, write

\|\boldsymbol{Z}\hat{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{-1}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|\leq{\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{-1}\|\|\boldsymbol{Z}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\hat{\boldsymbol{\rho}}\|}=\frac{\|\boldsymbol{Z}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\hat{\boldsymbol{\rho}}\|}{\min_{j}\hat{\rho}_{j}}.

By triangle inequality

\|\boldsymbol{Z}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\hat{\boldsymbol{\rho}}\|\leq\|\boldsymbol{Z}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\boldsymbol{\rho}\|+\|\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|\|\boldsymbol{\rho}-\hat{\boldsymbol{\rho}}\|.

Applying
\mathcal{E}_{1}
to the first term,
\mathcal{E}_{5}
to the second term, and
\mathcal{E}_{4}
to the denominator yields the first result. From the first result, Wedin’s
\sin\Theta
Theorem yields the second and third results while Weyl’s inequality yields the fourth result.
∎

Lemma A.2

(Eq. 43 of
[
Agarwal et al., 2021
]
)
.

Take
\lambda^{*}=\hat{s}_{k}
. Then

\hat{A}_{\cdot,j}=\frac{1}{\hat{\rho}_{j}}\varphi^{\boldsymbol{Z}\hat{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{-1}}_{\lambda^{*}}(Z_{\cdot,j}).

Lemma A.3
.

Suppose
k=r
, Assumptions
5.1
and
5.4
hold, and
\rho_{\min}>\frac{23\ln(np)}{n}
. Then

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}-\boldsymbol{A}}}\right\|^{2}_{2,\infty}~\Big|~\mathcal{E}

\displaystyle\leq C\bar{A}^{4}(K_{a}+\bar{K})^{2}(\kappa+\bar{K}+K_{a})^{2}\cdot\frac{r\ln^{5}(np)}{\rho_{\min}^{4}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{n}{p}+n\Delta_{E}^{2}}}\right).

Proof.

Fix a column index
j\in[p]
. Observe that

\hat{A}_{\cdot,j}-A_{\cdot,j}=\Big\{\hat{A}_{\cdot,j}-\varphi^{\boldsymbol{Z}\hat{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{-1}}_{\lambda^{*}}\big(A_{\cdot,j}\big)\Big\}+\Big\{\varphi^{\boldsymbol{Z}\hat{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{-1}}_{\lambda^{*}}\big(A_{\cdot,j}\big)-A_{\cdot,j}\Big\}.

Recall that
\varphi^{\boldsymbol{Z}\hat{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{-1}}_{\lambda^{*}}
projects onto the span of the top
r
left singular vectors
\{\hat{u}_{1},...,\hat{u}_{r}\}
of
\boldsymbol{Z}\bhrho^{-1}
, which are are also the top
r
left singular vectors of
\boldsymbol{Z}
since
\bhrho^{-1}
is diagonal.
Hence
\varphi^{\boldsymbol{Z}\hat{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{-1}}_{\lambda^{*}}(A_{\cdot,j})-A_{\cdot,j}\in span\{\hat{u}_{1},\ldots,\hat{u}_{r}\}^{\perp}.

By Lemma
A.2
,

\hat{A}_{\cdot,j}-\varphi^{\boldsymbol{Z}\hat{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{-1}}_{\lambda^{*}}(A_{\cdot,j})=\frac{1}{\hat{\rho}_{j}}\varphi^{\boldsymbol{Z}\hat{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{-1}}_{\lambda^{*}}(Z_{\cdot,j})-\varphi^{\boldsymbol{Z}\hat{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{-1}}_{\lambda^{*}}(A_{\cdot,j})\in span\{\hat{u}_{1},\ldots,\hat{u}_{r}\}.

Therefore

\Big\|\hat{A}_{\cdot,j}-A_{\cdot,j}\Big\|_{2}^{2}=\Big\|\hat{A}_{\cdot,j}-\varphi^{\boldsymbol{Z}\hat{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{-1}}_{\lambda^{*}}\big(A_{\cdot,j}\big)\Big\|_{2}^{2}+\Big\|\varphi^{\boldsymbol{Z}\hat{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{-1}}_{\lambda^{*}}\big(A_{\cdot,j}\big)-A_{\cdot,j}\Big\|_{2}^{2}.

Again applying Lemma
A.2
,

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|\hat{A}_{\cdot,j}-\varphi^{\boldsymbol{Z}\hat{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{-1}}_{\lambda^{*}}(A_{\cdot,j})}}\right\|_{2}^{2}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|\frac{1}{\hat{\rho}_{j}}\varphi^{\boldsymbol{Z}\hat{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{-1}}_{\lambda^{*}}(Z_{\cdot,j}-\rho_{j}A_{\cdot,j})+\frac{\rho_{j}-\hat{\rho}_{j}}{\hat{\rho}_{j}}\varphi^{\boldsymbol{Z}\hat{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{-1}}_{\lambda^{*}}(A_{\cdot,j})}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|\frac{1}{\hat{\rho}_{j}}\varphi^{\boldsymbol{Z}\hat{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{-1}}_{\lambda^{*}}(Z_{\cdot,j}-\rho_{j}A_{\cdot,j})}}\right\|_{2}^{2}+2\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|\frac{\rho_{j}-\hat{\rho}_{j}}{\hat{\rho}_{j}}\varphi^{\boldsymbol{Z}\hat{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{-1}}_{\lambda^{*}}(A_{\cdot,j})}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\delta^{2}}{\rho_{j}^{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|\varphi^{\boldsymbol{Z}\hat{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{-1}}_{\lambda^{*}}(Z_{\cdot,j}-\rho_{j}A_{\cdot,j})}}\right\|_{2}^{2}+C\frac{\delta^{2}}{\rho_{j}^{2}}\frac{\ln(np)}{n}\|A_{\cdot,j}\|_{2}^{2}

where the last line uses
\mathcal{E}_{4}
and
\mathcal{E}_{5}
. Note that

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|\varphi^{\boldsymbol{Z}\hat{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{-1}}_{\lambda^{*}}(Z_{\cdot,j}-\rho_{j}A_{\cdot,j})}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq 2\,\Big\|\varphi^{\boldsymbol{Z}\hat{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{-1}}_{\lambda^{*}}(Z_{\cdot,j}-\rho_{j}A_{\cdot,j})-\varphi^{\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}}(Z_{\cdot,j}-\rho_{j}A_{\cdot,j})\Big\|_{2}^{2}+2\,\Big\|\varphi^{\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}}(Z_{\cdot,j}-\rho_{j}A_{\cdot,j})\Big\|_{2}^{2}.

Since
k=r
,
\varphi^{\boldsymbol{Z}\hat{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{-1}}_{\lambda^{*}}(w)=\hat{\boldsymbol{U}}_{r}\hat{\boldsymbol{U}}_{r}^{T}w
and
\varphi^{\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}}(w)=\boldsymbol{U}\boldsymbol{U}^{T}w
for
w\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. By Lemma
A.1
,

\displaystyle\Big\|\varphi^{\boldsymbol{Z}\hat{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{-1}}_{\lambda^{*}}(Z_{\cdot,j}-\rho_{j}A_{\cdot,j})-\varphi^{\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}}(Z_{\cdot,j}-\rho_{j}A_{\cdot,j})\Big\|_{2}\leq\|\boldsymbol{U}\boldsymbol{U}^{T}-\hat{\boldsymbol{U}}_{r}\hat{\boldsymbol{U}}_{r}^{T}\|\big\|Z_{\cdot,j}-\rho_{j}A_{\cdot,j}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\delta}{\rho_{\min}s_{r}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((\sqrt{n}+\sqrt{p})\Delta_{H,op}+\|\boldsymbol{E}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|+\sqrt{\frac{\ln(np)}{n}}\|\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|}}\right)\big\|Z_{\cdot,j}-\rho_{j}A_{\cdot,j}\big\|_{2}.

Combining the inequalities together, we have
\Big\|\hat{A}_{\cdot,j}-\varphi^{\boldsymbol{Z}\hat{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{-1}}_{\lambda^{*}}\big(A_{\cdot,j}\big)\Big\|_{2}^{2}
is bounded by

\displaystyle\frac{C\delta^{4}}{\rho_{\min}^{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{(\sqrt{n}+\sqrt{p})\Delta_{H,op}}{\rho_{\min}s_{r}}+\frac{\|\boldsymbol{E}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|}{\rho_{\min}s_{r}}+\frac{\sqrt{\ln(np)/n}\|\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|}{\rho_{\min}s_{r}}}}\right)^{2}\big\|Z_{\cdot,j}-\rho_{j}A_{\cdot,j}\big\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{4\delta^{2}}{\rho_{\min}^{2}}\Big\|\varphi^{\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}}(Z_{\cdot,j}-\rho_{j}A_{\cdot,j})\Big\|_{2}^{2}+C\frac{\delta^{2}}{\rho_{\min}^{2}}\frac{\ln(np)}{n}\|A_{\cdot,j}\|_{2}^{2}.

Since

\boldsymbol{A}=\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}+\boldsymbol{E}^{\textsc{(lr)}}
,

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|\varphi^{\boldsymbol{Z}\hat{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{-1}}_{\lambda^{*}}\big(A_{\cdot,j}\big)-A_{\cdot,j}}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq 2\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|\varphi^{\boldsymbol{Z}\hat{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{-1}}_{\lambda^{*}}\big(A^{\textsc{(lr)}}_{\cdot,j}\big)-A^{\textsc{(lr)}}_{\cdot,j}}}\right\|_{2}^{2}+2\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|\varphi^{\boldsymbol{Z}\hat{\boldsymbol{\rho}}^{-1}}_{\lambda^{*}}\big(E^{\textsc{(lr)}}_{\cdot,j}\big)-E^{\textsc{(lr)}}_{\cdot,j}}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\,\|\boldsymbol{U}\boldsymbol{U}^{T}-\hat{\boldsymbol{U}}_{r}\hat{\boldsymbol{U}}_{r}^{T}\|^{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|A^{\textsc{(lr)}}_{\cdot,j}}}\right\|_{2}^{2}+2\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|E^{\textsc{(lr)}}_{\cdot,j}}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\delta^{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{(\sqrt{n}+\sqrt{p})\Delta_{H,op}}{\rho_{\min}s_{r}}+\frac{\|\boldsymbol{E}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|}{\rho_{\min}s_{r}}+\frac{\sqrt{\ln(np)/n}\|\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|}{\rho_{\min}s_{r}}}}\right)^{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|A^{\textsc{(lr)}}_{\cdot,j}}}\right\|_{2}^{2}+2\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|E^{\textsc{(lr)}}_{\cdot,j}}}\right\|_{2}^{2},

where the final inequality appeals to Lemma
A.1
.
To conclude, substitute the bounds on each term, appeal to
\mathcal{E}_{2}
and
\mathcal{E}_{3}
, then simplify using Assumptions
5.1
and
5.4
.
∎

Lemma A.4
.

Suppose Assumptions
5.1
,
5.2
, and
5.3
hold. Then

\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}-\boldsymbol{A}\|^{2}_{2,\infty}\1\{\mathcal{E}^{c}\}}}\right]\leq\Delta_{adv}\frac{1}{n^{2}p^{5}}
, where
\Delta_{adv}:=C\Big\{\bar{A}^{2}+K_{a}^{2}\ln^{2}(np)\Big\}.

Proof.

By Cauchy-Schwarz,
\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}-\boldsymbol{A}\|^{2}_{2,\infty}\1\{\mathcal{E}^{c}\}}}\right]\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}-\boldsymbol{A}\|^{4}_{2,\infty}}}\right]}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\1^{2}\{\mathcal{E}^{c}\}}}\right]}.

Within the first factor,

\max_{j\in[p]}\|\hat{A}_{\cdot,j}\|_{2}\leq\max_{j\in[p]}\frac{1}{\hat{\rho}_{j}}\|Z_{\cdot,j}\|_{2}\leq n\cdot\sqrt{n}(\bar{A}+\max_{i,j}|H_{ij}|),

so

\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}-\boldsymbol{A}\|^{4}_{2,\infty}}}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}[\{n^{\frac{3}{2}}(\bar{A}+\max_{i,j}|H_{ij}|)+\sqrt{n}\bar{A}\}^{4}]\leq Cn^{6}\{\bar{A}^{4}+K_{a}^{4}\ln^{4}(np)\}.

The final inequality holds since

\mathbb{E}[\max_{i,j}|H_{ij}|^{4}]\leq CK_{a}^{4}\ln^{\frac{4}{a}}(np).
Since
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}^{c})\leq\frac{C}{n^{10}p^{10}}
, we conclude that

\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}-\boldsymbol{A}\|^{2}_{2,\infty}\1\{\mathcal{E}^{c}\}}}\right]\leq C\sqrt{n^{6}(\bar{A}^{4}+K_{a}^{4}\ln^{4}(np))}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n^{10}p^{10}}}.

∎

Proof of Theorem
5.1
.

The result follows from Lemmas
A.3
and
A.4
. By the law of iterated expectations, results conditional on
\boldsymbol{A}
imply the same unconditional on
\boldsymbol{A}
.
∎

Appendix B
Error-in-variable regression

We write the proofs without nonlinear dictionaries for clarity.

Recall that the
fill
operator rescales using
\bhrho
calculated from
train
. Denote the SVDs

\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)},\textsc{train}}=\boldsymbol{U}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{V}^{T}
,
\textsc{fill}(\boldsymbol{Z}^{\textsc{train}})=\boldsymbol{Z}^{\textsc{train}}\bhrho^{-1}=\hat{\boldsymbol{U}}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}^{T}
,
\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\textsc{train}}=\hat{\boldsymbol{U}}_{k}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{k}\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k}^{T}.

In this notation,
\boldsymbol{V}
is an orthonormal basis for
\textsc{row}\{\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)},\textsc{train}}\}
.
Let
\boldsymbol{V}_{\perp}
be an orthonormal basis for its orthogonal complement. Likewise we define
\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k,\perp}
.
Define
s_{k}
and
\hat{s}_{k}
as the
k
-th singular values of
\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)},\textsc{train}}
and
\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\textsc{train}}
, respectively.
Next, denote the SVDs

\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)},\textsc{test}}=\boldsymbol{U}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{V}^{\prime})^{T}
,
\textsc{fill}(\boldsymbol{Z}^{\textsc{test}})=\boldsymbol{Z}^{\textsc{test}}\bhrho^{-1}=\hat{\boldsymbol{U}}^{\prime}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{\prime}(\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}^{\prime})^{T}
,
\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\textsc{test}}=\hat{\boldsymbol{U}}^{\prime}_{k}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{\prime}_{k}(\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}^{\prime}_{k})^{T}.

We define
\boldsymbol{V}^{\prime}_{\perp}
and
\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}^{\prime}_{\perp}
analogously to
\boldsymbol{V}_{\perp}
. Define
s^{\prime}_{k}
and
\hat{s}^{\prime}_{k}
as the
k
-th singular values of
\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)},\textsc{test}}
and
\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\textsc{test}}
, respectively.
Finally, denote the SVD of the row-wise concatenation of
\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)},\textsc{train}}
and
\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)},\textsc{test}}
as

\tilde{\boldsymbol{U}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{V}}^{T}.

We define
\tilde{\boldsymbol{V}}_{\perp}
analogously to
\boldsymbol{V}_{\perp}
but with respect to the row-wise concatenation of
\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)},\textsc{train}}
and
\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)},\textsc{test}}
.

We define
\beta^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
as the unique solution to the following optimization problem across
train
and
test
:

\min_{\beta\in\mathbb{R}^{p}}\|\beta\|_{2}
such that
\beta\in\argmin\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|\begin{bmatrix}\gamma_{0}(\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{train}})\\
\gamma_{0}(\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{test}})\end{bmatrix}-\begin{bmatrix}\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)},\textsc{train}}\\
\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)},\textsc{test}}\end{bmatrix}\beta}}\right\|_{2}^{2}.

\beta^{*}
is not the quantity of interest, but rather a theoretical device. It defines the unique, minimal-norm, low-rank, linear approximation to the regression
\gamma_{0}
.

Recall
Y_{i}=A^{\textsc{(lr)}}_{i,\cdot}\beta^{*}+\phi_{i}^{\textsc{(lr)}}+\varepsilon_{i}
.
Denote by
Y^{\textsc{train}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
the concatenation of
(Y_{i})_{i\in\textsc{train}}
. Likewise for
\varepsilon^{\textsc{train}}
and
\phi^{\textsc{(lr)},\textsc{train}}
. In the argument for
train error
, all objects correspond to
train
. For this reason, we suppress superscipt
train
when possible. For
i\in\textsc{test}
, let

\hat{\gamma}_{i}=Z_{i,\cdot}\bhrho^{-1}\hat{\beta}
and
\gamma_{i}=\gamma_{0}(A_{i,\cdot})

which form the vectors
\hat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}},\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

Define the events:

\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{1}\coloneqq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\textsc{test}}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{test}}\|^{2}_{2,\infty},\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\textsc{train}}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)},\textsc{train}}\|^{2}_{2,\infty},\leq\tilde{\Delta}_{1}}}\right\},

\tilde{\Delta}_{1}\coloneqq C_{1}\cdot\frac{r\ln^{5}(np)}{\rho_{\min}^{4}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{n}{p}+n\Delta^{2}_{E}}}\right)\quad\textrm{and}\quad C_{1}=C\bar{A}^{4}(K_{a}+\bar{K})^{2}(\kappa+\bar{K}+K_{a})^{2};

\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2}\coloneqq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\|\boldsymbol{Z}^{\textsc{test}}\bhrho^{-1}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)},\textsc{test}}\|^{2}\leq\tilde{\Delta}_{2}}}\right\},\quad\tilde{\Delta}_{2}\coloneqq C\bar{A}^{2}(\kappa+\bar{K}+K_{a})^{2}\frac{\ln^{3}(np)}{\rho^{2}_{\min}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(n+p+np\Delta_{E}^{2}}}\right);

\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{3}\coloneqq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\|\boldsymbol{V}\boldsymbol{V}^{T}-\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{r}\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{r}^{T}\|^{2},\|\boldsymbol{V}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{V}^{\prime})^{T}-\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}^{\prime}_{r}(\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}^{\prime}_{r})^{T}\|^{2}\leq\tilde{\Delta}_{3}}}\right\},\quad\tilde{\Delta}_{3}\coloneqq\frac{r}{np}\tilde{\Delta}_{2};

\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{4}\coloneqq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\hat{s}_{r}\gtrsim s_{r}}}\right\};

\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{5}\coloneqq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*}),\varepsilon\rangle\leq\tilde{\Delta}_{5}}}\right\},\quad\tilde{\Delta}_{5}\coloneqq C\bar{\sigma}^{2}\ln(np)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{r+\|\phi^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|_{2}+\|\beta^{*}\|_{1}(\sqrt{n}\bar{A}+\tilde{\Delta}_{1}^{1/2})}}\right\}.

The Online Appendix uses the results in Appendix
A
to show that if the conditions of Theorem
5.1
hold and

\rho_{\min}\gg\tilde{C}\sqrt{r}\ln^{\frac{3}{2}}(np)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\vee\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\vee\Delta_{E}}}\right)
, where
\tilde{C}:=C\bar{A}\Big(\kappa+\bar{K}+K_{a}\Big),

then
\mathbb{P}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{c})\leq\frac{C}{n^{10}p^{10}}
where
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}\coloneqq\cap^{5}_{k=1}\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{k}
.

Lemma B.1
.

If Assumption
5.6
holds then

\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k,\perp}^{T}\hat{\beta}=0
and
\boldsymbol{V}_{\perp}^{T}\beta^{*}=(\boldsymbol{V}_{\perp}^{\prime})^{T}\beta^{*}=0.

Proof.

We generalize
[
Agarwal et al., 2020a
, Property 4.1]
, using our new definition of
\beta^{*}
and noting that
\textsc{row}(\boldsymbol{V}^{T})=\textsc{row}\{(\boldsymbol{V}^{\prime})^{T}\}=\textsc{row}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{V}}^{T})
.
∎

Lemma B.2
.

Deterministically,

\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}\hat{\beta}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\beta^{*}\|^{2}_{2}\leq C\big\{\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|^{2}_{2,\infty}\|\beta^{*}\|^{2}_{1}\ \vee\ \|\phi^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|^{2}_{2}\ \vee\ \langle\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*}),\varepsilon\rangle\big\}.

Proof.

To begin, write

\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}\hat{\beta}-Y\|_{2}^{2}=\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}\hat{\beta}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\beta^{*}-\phi^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\varepsilon\|_{2}^{2}-2\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}\hat{\beta}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\beta^{*},\varepsilon\rangle+2\langle\phi^{\textsc{(lr)}},\varepsilon\rangle.

By optimality of
\hat{\beta}
, we have

\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}\hat{\beta}-Y\|_{2}^{2}\leq\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}\beta^{*}-Y\|_{2}^{2}=\|(\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}})\beta^{*}-\phi^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\varepsilon\|_{2}^{2}-2\langle(\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}})\beta^{*},\varepsilon\rangle+2\langle\phi^{\textsc{(lr)}},\varepsilon\rangle.

Combining these results,

\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}\hat{\beta}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\beta^{*}-\phi^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\|(\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}})\beta^{*}-\phi^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|_{2}^{2}+2\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*}),\varepsilon\rangle
. Moreover, since

\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}\hat{\beta}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\beta^{*}-\phi^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|_{2}^{2}=\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}\hat{\beta}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\beta^{*}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\phi^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|_{2}^{2}-2\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}\hat{\beta}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\beta^{*},\phi^{\textsc{(lr)}}\rangle
and
\|(\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}})\beta^{*}-\phi^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|_{2}^{2}=\|(\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}})\beta^{*}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\phi^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|_{2}^{2}-2\langle(\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}})\beta^{*},\phi^{\textsc{(lr)}}\rangle

we conclude that

\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}\hat{\beta}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\beta^{*}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\|(\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}})\beta^{*}\|_{2}^{2}+2\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*}),\phi^{\textsc{(lr)}}\rangle+2\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*}),\varepsilon\rangle.
By Cauchy-Schwarz and triangle inequalities,

\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*}),\phi^{\textsc{(lr)}}\rangle\leq(\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}\hat{\beta}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\beta^{*}\|_{2}+\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}\beta^{*}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\beta^{*}\|_{2})\cdot\|\phi^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|_{2}.

In summary, for
a=\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}\hat{\beta}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\beta^{*}\|^{2}_{2}
,
b=\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}\beta^{*}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\beta^{*}\|^{2}_{2}
, and
c=b+2\sqrt{b}\|\phi^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|_{2}+2\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*}),\varepsilon\rangle
, we have shown

a\leq 2\sqrt{a}\|\phi^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|_{2}+c
, which we now analyze. Since
a\geq 0
, there are three possible cases: (i)
c\geq 0
,
2\sqrt{a}\|\phi^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|_{2}\geq c
, so
a\leq 4\sqrt{a}\|\phi^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|_{2}
implies
a\leq 16\|\phi^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|_{2}^{2}
; (ii)
c\geq 0
,
2\sqrt{a}\|\phi^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|_{2}<c
, so
a\leq 2c
; (iii)
c<0
, so
a<2\sqrt{a}\|\phi^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|_{2}
implies
a<4\|\phi^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|_{2}^{2}.

The three cases imply

a\leq 2c\vee 16\|\phi^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|_{2}^{2}.

Finally, let
d\coloneqq 2b+4\sqrt{b}\|\phi^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|_{2}+2\|\phi^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|_{2}^{2}
.
Then

d=2\{\sqrt{b}+\|\phi^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|_{2}\}^{2}\leq 4\{b+\|\phi^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|^{2}_{2}\}.

Note
2c\leq d+4\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*}),\varepsilon\rangle
.
Together with the earlier results, this implies

a\leq C\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{b\ \vee\ \|\phi^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|^{2}_{2}\ \vee\ \langle\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*}),\varepsilon\rangle}}\right\}.

Finally note
b\leq\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|^{2}_{2,\infty}\|\beta^{*}\|^{2}_{1}
.
∎

Proposition B.1

(Projected
train error
)
.

Suppose conditions of Theorem
5.1
hold.
Further suppose Assumptions
5.5
and
5.6
hold. Let
k=r
and

\rho_{\min}\gg\tilde{C}\sqrt{r}\ln^{\frac{3}{2}}(np)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\vee\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\vee\Delta_{E}}}\right)
.
Then with probability at least
1-O\{(np)^{-10}\}
,
\|\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{r}\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{r}^{T}(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})\|^{2}_{2}
is bounded by

\displaystyle C\bar{A}^{4}(K_{a}+\bar{K})^{2}(\kappa+\bar{K}+K_{a})^{2}\frac{\bar{\sigma}^{2}}{\rho^{4}_{\min}}\cdot r\ln^{6}(np)\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\ \frac{1}{np}\|\phi^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|^{2}_{2}+\|\beta^{*}\|^{2}_{1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\sqrt{n}}{\|\beta^{*}\|_{1}np}+\frac{r}{np}+\frac{r}{p^{2}}+\frac{r}{p}\Delta_{E}^{2}}}\right)}}\right\}.

Proof.

We show that for any
k
,
\|\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k}\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k}^{T}(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{C}{\hat{s}_{k}^{2}}\Big\{\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|^{2}_{2,\infty}\|\beta^{*}\|^{2}_{1}\ \vee\ \|\phi^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|^{2}_{2}\ \vee\ \langle\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*}),\varepsilon\rangle\Big\}
. Appealing to
\mathbb{P}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{c})\leq\frac{C}{n^{10}p^{10}}
yields the result. Since
\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k}
is an isometry,
\|\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k}\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k}^{T}(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})\|_{2}^{2}=\|\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k}^{T}(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})\|_{2}^{2}
. Therefore
\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})\|_{2}^{2}=(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})^{T}\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{k}^{2}\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k}^{T}(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})\geq\hat{s}_{k}^{2}\|\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k}^{T}(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})\|_{2}^{2}.
Next, consider
\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})\|_{2}^{2}\leq 2\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}\hat{\beta}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\beta^{*}\|_{2}^{2}+2\|\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}-\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}\|_{2,\infty}^{2}\|\beta^{*}\|_{1}^{2}.
Combining,
\|\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k}\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k}^{T}(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{2}{\hat{s}_{k}^{2}}\Big\{\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}\hat{\beta}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\beta^{*}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}-\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}\|_{2,\infty}^{2}\|\beta^{*}\|_{1}^{2}\Big\}.
Bound
\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}\hat{\beta}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\beta^{*}\|^{2}_{2}
by Lemma
B.2
.
∎

Proposition B.2

(
train error
)
.

Suppose conditions of Proposition
B.1
hold. Then with probability at least
1-O\{(np)^{-10}\}
,
\|\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*}\|^{2}_{2}
is bounded by

\displaystyle C\bar{A}^{4}(K_{a}+\bar{K})^{2}(\kappa+\bar{K}+K_{a})^{2}\frac{\bar{\sigma}^{2}}{\rho^{4}_{\min}}\cdot r\ln^{6}(np)\cdot\Big\{\ \frac{1}{np}\|\phi^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|^{2}_{2}+\|\beta^{*}\|^{2}_{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{r}{n}+\frac{r}{p}+r\Delta_{E}^{2}}}\right)\Big\}.

Proof.

We show

\|\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*}\|^{2}_{2}\leq C\Big[\|\boldsymbol{V}\boldsymbol{V}^{T}-\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k}\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k}^{T}\|^{2}\|\beta^{*}\|^{2}_{2}+\frac{1}{\hat{s}_{k}^{2}}\Big\{\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|^{2}_{2,\infty}\|\beta^{*}\|^{2}_{1}\ \vee\ \|\phi^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|^{2}_{2}\ \vee\ \langle\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*}),\varepsilon\rangle\Big\}\Big].
Appealing to
\mathbb{P}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{c})\leq\frac{C}{n^{10}p^{10}}
yields the result.
Write
\|\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*}\|_{2}^{2}=\|\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k}\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k}^{T}(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})\|_{2}^{2}+\|\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k,\perp}\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k,\perp}^{T}(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})\|_{2}^{2}
. Proposition
B.1
bounds the former. By Lemma
B.1
, the latter equals
\|\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k,\perp}\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k,\perp}^{T}\beta^{*}\|_{2}^{2}=\|(\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k,\perp}\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k,\perp}^{T}\beta^{*}-\boldsymbol{V}_{\perp}\boldsymbol{V}_{\perp}^{T})\beta^{*}\|_{2}^{2}
, which we bound by
\|\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k,\perp}\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k,\perp}^{T}-\boldsymbol{V}_{\perp}\boldsymbol{V}_{\perp}^{T}\|^{2}\|\beta^{*}\|_{2}^{2}=\|\boldsymbol{V}\boldsymbol{V}^{T}-\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k}\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k}^{T}\|^{2}\|\beta^{*}\|_{2}^{2}
.
∎

Proposition B.3

(
test error
)
.

Let the conditions of Theorem
5.2
hold. Then
\mathbb{E}[\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\textsc{test}}\hat{\beta}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{test}}\beta^{*}\|_{2}^{2}\1\{\tilde{\mathcal{E}}\}]
is bounded by

\displaystyle C_{1}C_{2}\cdot\bar{\sigma}^{2}\cdot\frac{r^{3}\ln^{8}(np)}{\rho_{\min}^{6}}\|\beta^{*}\|^{2}_{1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{1+\frac{p}{n}+\frac{n}{p}+(n+p)\Delta_{E}^{2}+np\Delta_{E}^{4}}}\right\}+C_{2}\cdot\frac{r^{2}\ln^{3}(np)}{\rho_{\min}^{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\Delta^{2}_{E}}}\right)\|\phi^{\textsc{train}}\|_{2}^{2}.

Proof.

We show
\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\textsc{test}}\hat{\beta}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{test}}\beta^{*}\|_{2}^{2}\leq C\sum_{m=1}^{3}\Delta_{m}
where
\Delta_{1}:=\big\{\|\boldsymbol{Z}^{\textsc{test}}\bhrho^{-1}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)},\textsc{test}}\|^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)},\textsc{test}}\|^{2}\|\boldsymbol{V}\boldsymbol{V}^{T}-\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{r}\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{r}^{T}\|^{2}\big\}\|\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*}\|_{2}^{2}
,
\Delta_{2}:=\frac{\|\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)},\textsc{test}}\|^{2}}{\hat{s}_{r}^{2}}\Big\{\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\textsc{train}}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)},\textsc{train}}\|^{2}_{2,\infty}\|\beta^{*}\|^{2}_{1}\ \vee\ \|\phi^{\textsc{(lr)},\textsc{train}}\|^{2}_{2}\ \vee\ \langle\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\textsc{train}}(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*}),\varepsilon\rangle\Big\}
, and
\Delta_{3}:=\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\textsc{test}}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{test}}\|_{2,\infty}^{2}\|\beta^{*}\|_{1}^{2}
. Appealing to
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}
yields the result. To begin, write
\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\textsc{test}}\hat{\beta}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{test}}\beta^{*}\|_{2}^{2}\leq 2\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\textsc{test}}\big(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*}\big)\|_{2}^{2}+2\|(\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\textsc{test}}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{test}})\beta^{*}\|_{2}^{2}.
We bound the latter term by matrix Hölder:

\|(\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\textsc{test}}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{test}})\beta^{*}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\textsc{test}}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{test}}\|_{2,\infty}^{2}\|\beta^{*}\|_{1}^{2}
. In what remains, we analyze the former term using
\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\textsc{test}}\big(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*}\big)\|_{2}^{2}\leq 2\|\big\{\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\textsc{test}}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)},\textsc{test}}\}\big(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*}\big)\|_{2}^{2}+2\|\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)},\textsc{test}}\big(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*}\big)\|_{2}^{2}.

By Weyl’s inequality, we have

\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\textsc{test}}-\boldsymbol{Z}^{\textsc{test}}\bhrho^{-1}\|=\hat{s}^{\prime}_{r+1}=\hat{s}^{\prime}_{r+1}-s^{\prime}_{r+1}\leq\|\boldsymbol{Z}^{\textsc{test}}\bhrho^{-1}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)},\textsc{test}}\|.

In turn, this gives
\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\textsc{test}}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)},\textsc{test}}\|\leq 2\|\boldsymbol{Z}^{\textsc{test}}\bhrho^{-1}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)},\textsc{test}}\|
and hence

\|\big\{\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\textsc{test}}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)},\textsc{test}}\}\big(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*}\big)\|_{2}^{2}\leq 4\|\boldsymbol{Z}^{\textsc{test}}\bhrho^{-1}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)},\textsc{test}}\|^{2}\cdot\|\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*}\|_{2}^{2}.

Assumption
5.6
implies

(\boldsymbol{V}^{\prime})^{T}\boldsymbol{V}_{\perp}=0
and hence
\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)},\textsc{test}}\boldsymbol{V}_{\perp}\boldsymbol{V}_{\perp}^{T}=0
. As a result,

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)},\textsc{test}}\big(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*}\big)\|_{2}^{2}=\|\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)},\textsc{test}}(\boldsymbol{V}\boldsymbol{V}^{T}+\boldsymbol{V}_{\perp}\boldsymbol{V}_{\perp}^{T})\big(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*}\big)\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\|\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)},\textsc{test}}\boldsymbol{V}\boldsymbol{V}^{T}\big(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*}\big)\|_{2}^{2}\leq\|\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)},\textsc{test}}\|^{2}~\|\boldsymbol{V}\boldsymbol{V}^{T}\big(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*}\big)\|_{2}^{2}

where
\|\boldsymbol{V}\boldsymbol{V}^{T}\big(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*}\big)\|_{2}^{2}\leq 2\|\boldsymbol{V}\boldsymbol{V}^{T}-\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{r}\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{r}^{T}\|^{2}\|\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*}\|_{2}^{2}+2\|\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{r}\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{r}^{T}\big(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*}\big)\|_{2}^{2}.
Finally appeal to the proof of Proposition
B.1
.
∎

Proposition B.4

(Implicit cleaning)
.

Let the conditions of Theorem
5.2
hold. Then
\mathbb{E}[\|\boldsymbol{Z}^{\textsc{test}}\bhrho^{-1}\hat{\beta}-\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\textsc{test}}\hat{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}\1\{\tilde{\mathcal{E}}\}]
has the same bound as Proposition
B.3
.

Proof.

We show
\|\boldsymbol{Z}^{\textsc{test}}\bhrho^{-1}\hat{\beta}-\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\textsc{test}}\hat{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}\leq C\|\boldsymbol{Z}^{\textsc{test}}\bhrho^{-1}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)},\textsc{test}}\|^{2}\cdot\big\{\|\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}^{\prime}_{r}(\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}^{\prime}_{r})^{T}-\boldsymbol{V}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{V}^{\prime})^{T}\|^{2}\|\beta^{*}\|^{2}_{2}\big\}.
Appealing to
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}
yields the result. Using the definitions

\boldsymbol{Z}^{\textsc{test}}\bhrho^{-1}=\hat{\boldsymbol{U}}^{\prime}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{\prime}(\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}^{\prime})^{T}
,
\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\textsc{test}}=\hat{\boldsymbol{U}}^{\prime}_{r}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{\prime}_{r}(\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}^{\prime}_{r})^{T}
, and
\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\textsc{test}}_{\perp}=\hat{\boldsymbol{U}}^{\prime}_{r,\perp}\hat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{\prime}_{r,\perp}(\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}^{\prime}_{r,\perp})^{T}
, write
\boldsymbol{Z}^{\textsc{test}}\bhrho^{-1}=\quad\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\textsc{test}}+\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\textsc{test}}_{\perp}
. Therefore
\|\boldsymbol{Z}^{\textsc{test}}\bhrho^{-1}\hat{\beta}-\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\textsc{test}}\hat{\beta}\|_{2}\leq\|\hat{\boldsymbol{U}}^{\prime}_{r,\perp}\|\cdot\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{\prime}_{r,\perp}\|\cdot\|(\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}^{\prime}_{r,\perp})^{T}\hat{\beta}\|_{2}=\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{\prime}_{r,\perp}\|\cdot\|(\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}^{\prime}_{r,\perp})^{T}\hat{\beta}\|_{2}.
By Weyl’s inequality,
\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{\prime}_{r,\perp}\|=\hat{s}^{\prime}_{r+1}=\hat{s}^{\prime}_{r+1}-s^{\prime}_{r+1}\leq\|\boldsymbol{Z}^{\textsc{test}}\bhrho^{-1}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)},\textsc{test}}\|.
Moreover,
\|(\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}^{\prime}_{r,\perp})^{T}\hat{\beta}\|_{2}=\|\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}^{\prime}_{r,\perp}(\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}^{\prime}_{r,\perp})^{T}\hat{\beta}\|_{2}\leq\|\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}^{\prime}_{r,\perp}(\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}^{\prime}_{r,\perp})^{T}(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})\|_{2}+\|\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}^{\prime}_{r,\perp}(\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}^{\prime}_{r,\perp})^{T}\beta^{*}\|_{2}.
Focusing on the former term,

\|\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}^{\prime}_{r,\perp}(\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}^{\prime}_{r,\perp})^{T}(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})\|_{2}\leq\|\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*}\|_{2}.
By Lemma
B.1
, the latter term equals

\displaystyle\|\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}^{\prime}_{r,\perp}(\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}^{\prime}_{r,\perp})^{T}\boldsymbol{V}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{V}^{\prime})^{T}\beta^{*}\|_{2}\leq\|\{\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}^{\prime}_{r,\perp}(\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}^{\prime}_{r,\perp})^{T}-\boldsymbol{V}^{\prime}_{\perp}(\boldsymbol{V}^{\prime}_{\perp})^{T}\}\boldsymbol{V}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{V}^{\prime})^{T}\beta^{*}\|_{2}+\|\boldsymbol{V}^{\prime}_{\perp}(\boldsymbol{V}^{\prime}_{\perp})^{T}\boldsymbol{V}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{V}^{\prime})^{T}\beta^{*}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\|\{\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}^{\prime}_{r,\perp}(\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}^{\prime}_{r,\perp})^{T}-\boldsymbol{V}^{\prime}_{\perp}(\boldsymbol{V}^{\prime}_{\perp})^{T}\}\boldsymbol{V}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{V}^{\prime})^{T}\beta^{*}\|_{2}=\|\{\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}^{\prime}_{r,\perp}(\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}^{\prime}_{r,\perp})^{T}-\boldsymbol{V}^{\prime}_{\perp}(\boldsymbol{V}^{\prime}_{\perp})^{T}\}\beta^{*}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\|\{\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}^{\prime}_{r}(\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}^{\prime}_{r})^{T}-\boldsymbol{V}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{V}^{\prime})^{T}\}\beta^{*}\|_{2}\leq\|\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}^{\prime}_{r}(\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}^{\prime}_{r})^{T}-\boldsymbol{V}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{V}^{\prime})^{T}\|\|\beta^{*}\|_{2},

which is dominated by the former term by the proof of Proposition
B.2
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
5.2
.

To begin, write

\mathbb{E}\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}-\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{0}\|_{2}^{2}\leq 2\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\|\boldsymbol{Z}^{\textsc{test}}\bhrho^{-1}\hat{\beta}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{test}}\beta^{*}\|_{2}^{2}\1\{\tilde{\mathcal{E}}\}}}\right]+2\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\|\boldsymbol{Z}^{\textsc{test}}\bhrho^{-1}\hat{\beta}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{test}}\beta^{*}\|_{2}^{2}\1\{\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{c}\}}}\right]+2\|\phi^{\textsc{test}}\|_{2}^{2}.
Consider the first term. Using the bound

\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\|\boldsymbol{Z}^{\textsc{test}}\bhrho^{-1}\hat{\beta}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{test}}\beta^{*}\|_{2}^{2}\1\{\tilde{\mathcal{E}}\}}}\right]\leq 2\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\|\boldsymbol{Z}^{\textsc{test}}\bhrho^{-1}\hat{\beta}-\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\textsc{test}}\hat{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}\1\{\tilde{\mathcal{E}}\}}}\right]\\
+2\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\textsc{test}}\hat{\beta}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{test}}\beta^{*}\|_{2}^{2}\1\{\tilde{\mathcal{E}}\}}}\right],

we may appeal to Propositions
B.3
and
B.4
. Consider the second term. Write

\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\|\boldsymbol{Z}^{\textsc{test}}\bhrho^{-1}\hat{\beta}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{test}}\beta^{*}\|_{2}^{2}\1\{\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{c}\}}}\right]\leq 2\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\|\boldsymbol{Z}^{\textsc{test}}\bhrho^{-1}\hat{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}\1\{\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{c}\}}}\right]+2\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\|\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{test}}\beta^{*}\|_{2}^{2}\1\{\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{c}\}}}\right].

Since
\|\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{test}}\beta^{*}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\|\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{test}}\|^{2}_{2,\infty}\|\beta^{*}\|^{2}_{1}\leq n\bar{A}^{2}\|\beta^{*}\|^{2}_{1}
, we have that
\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\|\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{test}}\beta^{*}\|_{2}^{2}\1\{\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{c}\}}}\right]\leq n\bar{A}^{2}\|\beta^{*}\|^{2}_{1}\mathbb{P}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{c})
. By Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\mathbb{E}\big[\|\boldsymbol{Z}^{\textsc{test}}\bhrho^{-1}\hat{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}\1\{\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{c}\}\big]\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\big[\|\boldsymbol{Z}^{\textsc{test}}\bhrho^{-1}\hat{\beta}\|_{2}^{4}\big]}\sqrt{\mathbb{P}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{c})}.

These expressions are dominated by the bound from Proposition
B.3
under Assumption
5.7
.
∎

Appendix C
Error-in-variable balancing weight

As before, we write the proofs without nonlinear dictionaries for clarity.

We define
\eta^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
as the unique solution to the following optimization problem across
train
and
test
:

\min_{\eta\in\mathbb{R}^{p}}\|\eta\|_{2}
such that
\eta\in\argmin\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|\begin{bmatrix}\alpha_{0}(\boldsymbol{W}^{\textsc{train}})\\
\alpha_{0}(\boldsymbol{W}^{\textsc{test}})\end{bmatrix}-\begin{bmatrix}\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)},\textsc{train}}\\
\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)},\textsc{test}}\end{bmatrix}\eta}}\right\|_{2}^{2}.

\eta^{*}
is not the quantity of interest, but rather a theoretical device. It defines the unique, minimal-norm, low-rank, linear approximation to the balancing weight
\alpha_{0}
.

\hat{M}
is the counterfactual moment. Write
\hat{\boldsymbol{G}}=\frac{1}{n}(\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\textsc{train}})^{T}\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\textsc{train}}
as the covariance matrix after data cleaning. In this notation,
\hat{\boldsymbol{G}}\hat{\eta}=\hat{M}^{T}
, and these feasible objects are computed from
train
. We analogously define
M^{*}
and
\boldsymbol{G}^{*}
using the low rank approximation to the signal. In this notation,
\boldsymbol{G}^{*}\eta^{*}=(M^{*})^{T}
, and these infeasible objects are defined from
train
and
test
. See the Online Appendix for a more formal statement. Finally, let
\Delta_{RR}:=n\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\|\hat{M}^{T}-(M^{*})^{T}\|_{\max}+\|\boldsymbol{G}^{*}-\hat{\boldsymbol{G}}\|_{\max}\|\eta^{*}\|_{1}}}\right\}.

Define the event:
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{5}\coloneqq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\Delta_{RR}\leq\tilde{\Delta}_{5}}}\right\}
where
\tilde{\Delta}_{5}\coloneqq C\bar{A}^{5}(\sqrt{C_{m}^{\prime}}+C_{m}^{\prime\prime}+\bar{\alpha}+\bar{A})\frac{(K_{a}+\bar{K})^{2}(\kappa+\bar{K}+K_{a})^{2}}{\rho^{4}_{\min}}r\cdot\ln^{5}(np)\cdot n\|\eta^{*}\|_{1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{n}+\frac{1}{p}+\frac{n}{p^{2}}+\Delta^{2}_{E}+n\Delta_{E}^{4}}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.
Set
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}\coloneqq\cap^{5}_{k=1}\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{k}
where the remaining events are defined in Appendix
B
. The Online Appendix shows that if the conditions of Theorem
5.3
hold, then
\mathbb{P}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{c})\leq\frac{C}{n^{10}p^{10}}
.

Lemma C.1
.

If Assumptions
5.6
and
5.8
hold,

\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k,\perp}^{T}\hat{\eta}=0
and
\boldsymbol{V}_{\perp}^{T}\eta^{*}=(\boldsymbol{V}_{\perp}^{\prime})^{T}\eta^{*}=0
.

Proof.

For the former result,
\hat{\eta}
is the unique solution to the program

\min_{\eta\in\mathbb{R}^{p}}\|\eta\|_{2}
such that
\eta\in\argmin-2\hat{M}\eta+\eta^{T}\hat{\boldsymbol{G}}\eta

where
\hat{M}\in\textsc{row}(\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\textsc{train}})
by Assumption
5.8
and
\textsc{row}(\hat{\boldsymbol{G}})=\textsc{row}\{(\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\textsc{train}})^{T}\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\textsc{train}}\}=\textsc{row}(\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\textsc{train}})
. Therefore
\hat{\eta}\in\textsc{row}(\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\textsc{train}})
, so we can appeal to the same reasoning as Lemma
B.1
. The latter result is similar.
∎

Lemma C.2
.

\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}\hat{\eta}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\eta^{*}\|_{2}^{2}\leq C\big\{\|\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k}\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k}^{T}(\hat{\eta}-\eta^{*})\|_{1}\cdot\Delta_{RR}\vee\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|^{2}_{2,\infty}\|\eta^{*}\|_{1}^{2}\big\}
.

Proof.

To begin, write
\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}\hat{\eta}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\eta^{*}\|_{2}^{2}\leq 2\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}(\hat{\eta}-\eta^{*})\|_{2}^{2}+2\|(\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}})\eta^{*}\|_{2}^{2}.
We bound the latter term by
\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|^{2}_{2,\infty}\|\eta^{*}\|_{1}^{2}.
Hereafter, we focus on the former term:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}(\hat{\eta}-\eta^{*})\|_{2}^{2}=\frac{1}{n}(\hat{\eta}-\eta^{*})^{T}\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}^{T}\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}(\hat{\eta}-\eta^{*})=(\hat{\eta}-\eta^{*})^{T}\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k}\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k}^{T}\hat{\boldsymbol{G}}(\hat{\eta}-\eta^{*})

which is bounded by
\|\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k}\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k}^{T}(\hat{\eta}-\eta^{*})\|_{1}\cdot\|\hat{\boldsymbol{G}}(\hat{\eta}-\eta^{*})\|_{\max}.

Using the first order conditions,

\displaystyle\|\hat{\boldsymbol{G}}(\hat{\eta}-\eta^{*})\|_{\max}\leq\|\hat{\boldsymbol{G}}\hat{\eta}-\hat{M}^{T}\|_{\max}+\|\hat{M}^{T}-(M^{*})^{T}\|_{\max}+\|(M^{*})^{T}-\boldsymbol{G}^{*}\eta^{*}\|_{\max}+\|\boldsymbol{G}^{*}\eta^{*}-\hat{\boldsymbol{G}}\eta^{*}\|_{\max}

\displaystyle=0+\|\hat{M}^{T}-(M^{*})^{T}\|_{\max}+0+\|\boldsymbol{G}^{*}-\hat{\boldsymbol{G}}\|_{\max}\|\eta^{*}\|_{1}.\qed

Proposition C.1

(Projected
train error
)
.

Suppose the conditions of Theorem
5.1
hold.
Further suppose Assumptions
5.6
,
5.8
,
5.9
, and
5.10
hold, and
\|\alpha_{0}\|_{\infty}\leq\bar{\alpha}
. Let
k=r
and

\rho_{\min}\gg\tilde{C}\sqrt{r}\ln^{\frac{3}{2}}(np)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\vee\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\vee\Delta_{E}}}\right)
, where
\tilde{C}:=C\bar{A}\Big(\kappa+\bar{K}+K_{a}\Big).

Then with probability at least
1-O\{(np)^{-10}\}
,
\|\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{r}\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{r}^{T}(\hat{\eta}-\eta^{*})\|^{2}_{2}
is bounded by

\displaystyle C\bar{A}^{10}(\sqrt{C_{m}^{\prime}}+C_{m}^{\prime\prime}+\bar{\alpha}+\bar{A})^{2}(K_{a}+\bar{K})^{4}(\kappa+\bar{K}+K_{a})^{4}\cdot r^{4}\cdot\ln^{10}(np)\cdot\frac{\|\eta^{*}\|^{2}_{1}}{\rho^{8}_{\min}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{np}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}+\frac{n}{p^{3}}+\frac{1}{p}\Delta^{2}_{E}+\frac{n}{p}\Delta_{E}^{4}}}\right).

Proof.

We show that for any
k
,
\|\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k}\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k}^{T}(\hat{\eta}-\eta^{*})\|_{2}^{2}\leq C\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\frac{1}{\hat{s}_{k}^{2}}\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|^{2}_{2,\infty}\|\eta^{*}\|^{2}_{1}+\frac{1}{\hat{s}_{k}^{4}}p\cdot\Delta^{2}_{RR}}}\right\}.
Appealing to
\mathbb{P}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{c})\leq\frac{C}{n^{10}p^{10}}
yields the result. As in the proof of Proposition
B.1
,
\|\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k}\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k}^{T}(\hat{\eta}-\eta^{*})\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{2}{\hat{s}_{k}^{2}}\Big\{\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}\hat{\eta}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\eta^{*}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}-\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}\|_{2,\infty}^{2}\|\eta^{*}\|_{1}^{2}\Big\}.

Using Lemma
C.2
, we conclude that

\|\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k}\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k}^{T}(\hat{\eta}-\eta^{*})\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{C}{\hat{s}_{k}^{2}}\Big\{\|\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k}\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k}^{T}(\hat{\eta}-\eta^{*})\|_{1}\cdot\Delta_{RR}\vee\|\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}-\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}\|_{2,\infty}^{2}\|\eta^{*}\|_{1}^{2}\Big\}.

There are two cases. In the first case,
\|\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k}\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k}^{T}(\hat{\eta}-\eta^{*})\|_{2}^{2}\leq C\frac{1}{\hat{s}_{k}^{2}}\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|^{2}_{2,\infty}\|\eta^{*}\|^{2}_{1}
, giving the first term. In the second case,
\|\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k}\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k}^{T}(\hat{\eta}-\eta^{*})\|_{2}^{2}\leq C\frac{1}{\hat{s}_{k}^{2}}\|\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k}\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k}^{T}(\hat{\eta}-\eta^{*})\|_{1}\cdot\Delta_{RR}
. Bounding
\|\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k}\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k}^{T}(\hat{\eta}-\eta^{*})\|_{1}\leq\sqrt{p}\|\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k}\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k}^{T}(\hat{\eta}-\eta^{*})\|_{2}
, dividing both sides by
\|\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k}\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k}^{T}(\hat{\eta}-\eta^{*})\|_{2}
, and squaring gives the second term.
∎

Proposition C.2

(
train error
)
.

Suppose the conditions of Proposition
C.1
hold.
Then with probability at least
1-O\{(np)^{-10}\}
,
\|\hat{\eta}-\eta^{*}\|^{2}_{2}
is bounded by

\displaystyle C\bar{A}^{10}(\sqrt{C_{m}^{\prime}}+C_{m}^{\prime\prime}+\bar{\alpha}+\bar{A})^{2}(K_{a}+\bar{K})^{4}(\kappa+\bar{K}+K_{a})^{4}\cdot r^{4}\cdot\ln^{10}(np)\cdot\frac{\|\eta^{*}\|^{2}_{2}}{\rho^{8}_{\min}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{n}+\frac{1}{p}+\frac{n}{p^{2}}+\Delta^{2}_{E}+n\Delta_{E}^{4}}}\right).

Proof.

We show
\|\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k}\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k}^{T}(\hat{\eta}-\eta^{*})\|_{2}^{2}\leq C\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\frac{1}{\hat{s}_{k}^{2}}\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|^{2}_{2,\infty}\|\eta^{*}\|^{2}_{1}+\frac{1}{\hat{s}_{k}^{4}}p\cdot\Delta^{2}_{RR}}}\right\}.
Appealing to
\mathbb{P}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{c})\leq\frac{C}{n^{10}p^{10}}
yields the result. The argument is similar to Proposition
B.2
, replacing Lemma
B.1
with Lemma
C.1
and Proposition
B.1
with Proposition
C.1
.
∎

Proposition C.3

(
test error
)
.

Let the conditions of Theorem
5.3
hold. Then
\mathbb{E}[\|\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\textsc{test}}\hat{\eta}-\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{test}}\eta^{*}\|_{2}^{2}\1\{\tilde{\mathcal{E}}\}]
is bounded by

\displaystyle C_{3}\cdot\frac{r^{5}\ln^{13}(np)}{\rho^{10}_{\min}}\cdot\|\eta^{*}\|^{2}_{1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{1+\frac{p}{n}+\frac{n}{p}+\frac{n^{2}}{p^{2}}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(n+p+\frac{n^{2}}{p}}}\right)\Delta^{2}_{E}+(np+n^{2})\Delta_{E}^{4}+n^{2}p\Delta^{6}_{E}}}\right\}.

Proof.

The proof is similar to Proposition
B.3
, updating
\Delta_{2}=\frac{\|\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)},\textsc{test}}\|^{2}}{\hat{s}_{r}^{2}}\Big\{\|\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k}\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{k}^{T}(\hat{\eta}-\eta^{*})\|_{1}\cdot\Delta_{RR}\vee\|\boldsymbol{A}^{\textsc{(lr)},\textsc{train}}-\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\textsc{train}}\|_{2,\infty}^{2}\|\eta^{*}\|_{1}^{2}\Big\}
. In particular, we bound
\|\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{r}\hat{\boldsymbol{V}}_{r}^{T}\big(\hat{\eta}-\eta^{*}\big)\|_{2}^{2}
using Proposition
C.1
instead of Proposition
B.1
.
∎

Proposition C.4

(Implicit cleaning)
.

Let the conditions of Theorem
5.3
hold. Then
\mathbb{E}[\|\boldsymbol{Z}^{\textsc{test}}\bhrho^{-1}\hat{\eta}-\hat{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\textsc{test}}\hat{\eta}\|_{2}^{2}\1\{\tilde{\mathcal{E}}\}]
has the same bound as Proposition
C.3
.

Proof.

The proof is analogous to Proposition
B.4
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
5.3
.

The proof is analogous to Theorem
5.2
, instead appealing to Propositions
C.3
and
C.4
.
∎

Appendix D
Data cleaning-adjusted confidence intervals

Let

\psi_{i}=\psi(W_{i,\cdot},\theta_{i},\gamma_{0},\alpha_{0})
where
\psi(w,\theta,\gamma,\alpha)=m(w,\gamma)+\alpha(w)\{y-\gamma(w)\}-\theta

and
\gamma\mapsto m(w,\gamma)
is linear.
We take as given that
(\gamma_{0},\alpha_{0})
exist, though the latter is implied by Assumption
5.10
. The Gateaux derivative of
\psi(w,\theta,\gamma,\alpha)
with respect to its argument
\gamma
in the direction
u
is

\{\partial_{\gamma}\psi(w,\theta,\gamma,\alpha)\}(u)=\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\psi(w,\theta,\gamma+\tau u,\alpha)\bigg|_{\tau=0}.

Let
L
be the number of folds, with fold
\ell
indexed by
I_{\ell}
. Train
(\hat{\gamma}_{\ell},\hat{\alpha}_{\ell})
on observations in
I_{\ell}^{c}
, which serves as
train
. Let
m=|I_{\ell}|=n/L
be the number of observations in
I_{\ell}
, which serves as
test
. Denote by
\mathbb{E}_{\ell}[\cdot]
the average over observations in
I_{\ell}
. This generalized notation allows us to reverse the roles of
train
and
test
, and to allow for more than two folds. The target and oracle are

\hat{\theta}=L^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\mathbb{E}_{\ell}[m(W_{i,\cdot},\hat{\gamma}_{\ell})+\hat{\alpha}_{\ell}(W_{i,\cdot})\{Y_{i}-\hat{\gamma}_{\ell}(W_{i,\cdot})\}]
and
\bar{\theta}=L^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\mathbb{E}_{\ell}[m(W_{i,\cdot},\gamma_{0})+\alpha_{0}(W_{i,\cdot})\{Y_{i}-\gamma_{0}(W_{i,\cdot})\}]
. For
i\in I_{\ell}
, let
\hat{\psi}_{i}=\psi(W_{i,\cdot},\hat{\theta},\hat{\gamma}_{\ell},\hat{\alpha}_{\ell})
.

Lemma D.1
.

Suppose Assumptions
5.9
and
5.10
hold,

\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_{i}^{2}|W_{i,\cdot}]\leq\bar{\sigma}^{2}
,
\|\alpha_{0}\|_{\infty}\leq\bar{\alpha}
, and that for
(i,j)\in I_{\ell}
,

\hat{\gamma}_{\ell}(W_{i,\cdot})\raisebox{0.51114pt}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{$\models$}}\hat{\gamma}_{\ell}(W_{j,\cdot})|I^{c}_{\ell}
and
\hat{\alpha}_{\ell}(W_{i,\cdot})\raisebox{0.51114pt}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{$\models$}}\hat{\alpha}_{\ell}(W_{j,\cdot})|I^{c}_{\ell}.

Then with probability
1-\epsilon
,

\frac{n^{1/2}}{\sigma}|\hat{\theta}-\bar{\theta}|\leq\Delta=\frac{3L}{\epsilon\sigma}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[(\bar{Q}^{1/2}+\bar{\alpha})\{\mathcal{R}(\hat{\gamma}_{\ell})\}^{\bar{q}/2}+\bar{\sigma}\{\mathcal{R}(\hat{\alpha}_{\ell})\}^{1/2}+\{n\mathcal{R}(\hat{\gamma}_{\ell})\mathcal{R}(\hat{\alpha}_{\ell})\}^{1/2}}}\right].

Proof.

We generalize
[
Chernozhukov et al., 2023
, Proposition S6]
to the new norm, noting that Jensen’s inequality and
\bar{q}\in(0,1]
imply that

\mathbb{E}_{\ell}[\mathbb{E}[m(W_{i,\cdot},u)^{2}|I^{c}_{\ell}]]\leq\bar{Q}\mathbb{E}_{\ell}[\{\mathbb{E}[u(W_{i})^{2}|I^{c}_{\ell}]\}^{\bar{q}}]\leq\bar{Q}\{\mathbb{E}_{\ell}[\mathbb{E}[u(W_{i})^{2}|I^{c}_{\ell}]]\}^{\bar{q}}
.
Moreover, using the shorthand
u_{i}=u(W_{i,\cdot})
and
v_{i}=v(W_{i,\cdot})
,

\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}_{\ell}\{|u(W_{i,\cdot})v(W_{i,\cdot})|\}]=\frac{1}{m}\mathbb{E}[u^{T}v]\leq\frac{1}{m}(\mathbb{E}[u^{T}u])^{1/2}(\mathbb{E}[v^{T}v])^{1/2}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{m}\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}_{\ell}[u_{i}^{2}]]}}\right)^{1/2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{m}\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}_{\ell}[v_{i}^{2}]]}}\right)^{1/2}=\sqrt{\mathcal{R}(\hat{\gamma}_{\ell})}\sqrt{\mathcal{R}(\hat{\alpha}_{\ell})}.

∎

Proof of Theorem
5.4
.

We generalize
[
Chernozhukov et al., 2023
, Theorem 1]
using Lemma
D.1
and an i.n.i.d. Berry Esseen lemma for
\bar{\theta}-\theta_{0}=\mathbb{E}_{n}\psi_{i}

[
Shevtsova, 2010
]
.
∎

Lemma D.2
.

Suppose Assumptions
5.9
and
5.10
hold,

\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_{i}^{2}|W_{i,\cdot}]\leq\bar{\sigma}^{2}
, and
\|\hat{\alpha}_{\ell}\|_{\infty}\leq\bar{\alpha}^{\prime}
. Then with probability
1-\epsilon^{\prime}/2
,

\mathbb{E}_{n}\{(\hat{\psi}_{i}-\psi_{i}+\theta_{0}-\theta_{i})^{2}\}\leq\Delta^{\prime}=4(\hat{\theta}-\theta_{0})^{2}+\frac{24L}{\epsilon^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\{\bar{Q}+(\bar{\alpha}^{\prime})^{2}\}\mathcal{R}(\hat{\gamma}_{\ell})^{\bar{q}}+\bar{\sigma}^{2}\mathcal{R}(\hat{\alpha}_{\ell})}}\right].

Proof.

We generalize
[
Chernozhukov et al., 2023
, Proposition S10]
to the new norm, appealing to Jensen’s inequality and
\bar{q}\in(0,1]
as in the proof of Lemma
D.1
.
∎

Lemma D.3
.

With probability
1-\epsilon^{\prime}/2
,

|\mathbb{E}_{n}(\psi_{i}^{2})-\sigma^{2}|\leq\Delta^{\prime\prime}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2}{\epsilon^{\prime}}}}\right)^{1/2}\frac{\chi^{2}}{n^{1/2}}.

Proof.

Let

B_{i}=\psi_{i}^{2}
and
\bar{B}=\mathbb{E}_{n}[B_{i}]

so that

\mathbb{E}[\bar{B}]=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[B_{i}]=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{i}^{2}=\sigma^{2}
and
\mathbb{V}[\bar{B}]=\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{V}(B_{i})}{n^{2}}\leq\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[B_{i}^{2}]}{n^{2}}=\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\chi^{4}_{i}}{n^{2}}=\frac{\chi^{4}}{n}.

By Markov inequality

\mathbb{P}(|\bar{B}-\mathbb{E}[\bar{B}]|>t)\leq\frac{\mathbb{V}[\bar{B}]}{t^{2}}=\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}}{2}.

Solving gives

t=\Delta^{\prime\prime}.

∎

Proof of Theorem
5.5
.

To begin, write

\hat{\sigma}^{2}-(\sigma^{2}+\textsc{bias})=\{\hat{\sigma}^{2}-\mathbb{E}_{n}(\psi_{i}^{2})-\textsc{bias}\}+\{\mathbb{E}_{n}(\psi_{i}^{2})-\sigma^{2}\}\leq\{\hat{\sigma}^{2}-\mathbb{E}_{n}(\psi_{i}^{2})-\textsc{bias}\}+\Delta^{\prime\prime}

where the inequality holds with probability
1-\epsilon^{\prime}/2
by Lemma
D.3
. In what follows, we focus on the former term. In particular, we write

\hat{\sigma}^{2}=\mathbb{E}_{n}\{(\hat{\psi}_{i}-\psi_{i})^{2}\}+2\mathbb{E}_{n}\{(\hat{\psi}_{i}-\psi_{i})\psi_{i}\}+\mathbb{E}_{n}(\psi_{i}^{2}),
then solve for
\textsc{bias}=\textsc{bias}_{1}+\textsc{bias}_{2}
as a function of
\Delta_{\textsc{out}}
in the decomposition

\hat{\sigma}^{2}-\mathbb{E}_{n}(\psi_{i}^{2})-\textsc{bias}=\mathbb{E}_{n}\{(\hat{\psi}_{i}-\psi_{i})^{2}\}-\textsc{bias}_{1}+2\mathbb{E}_{n}\{(\hat{\psi}_{i}-\psi_{i})\psi_{i}\}-\textsc{bias}_{2}.

To derive
\textsc{bias}_{1}
, open the square and write

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{n}\{(\hat{\psi}_{i}-\psi_{i})^{2}\}=\mathbb{E}_{n}\{(\hat{\psi}_{i}-\psi_{i}+\theta_{0}-\theta_{i})^{2}\}+\mathbb{E}_{n}\{(\theta_{i}-\theta_{0})^{2}\}+2\mathbb{E}_{n}\{(\hat{\psi}_{i}-\psi_{i}+\theta_{0}-\theta_{i})(\theta_{i}-\theta_{0})\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{n}\{(\hat{\psi}_{i}-\psi_{i}+\theta_{0}-\theta_{i})^{2}\}+\mathbb{E}_{n}\{(\theta_{i}-\theta_{0})^{2}\}+2[\mathbb{E}_{n}\{(\hat{\psi}_{i}-\psi_{i}+\theta_{0}-\theta_{i})^{2}\}]^{1/2}[\mathbb{E}_{n}\{(\theta_{i}-\theta_{0})^{2}\}]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\Delta^{\prime}+\Delta_{\textsc{out}}+2(\Delta^{\prime})^{1/2}\Delta_{\textsc{out}}^{1/2}

where the last line holds with probability
1-\epsilon^{\prime}/2
by Lemma
D.2
. Taking
\textsc{bias}_{1}=\Delta_{\textsc{out}}
, we have shown

\mathbb{E}_{n}\{(\hat{\psi}_{i}-\psi_{i})^{2}\}-\textsc{bias}_{1}\leq\Delta^{\prime}+2(\Delta^{\prime})^{1/2}\Delta_{\textsc{out}}^{1/2}.

To derive
\textsc{bias}_{2}
, write

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{n}\{(\hat{\psi}_{i}-\psi_{i})\psi_{i}\}\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathbb{E}_{n}\{(\hat{\psi}_{i}-\psi_{i})^{2}\}}}\right]^{1/2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{|\mathbb{E}_{n}(\psi_{i}^{2})-\sigma^{2}|+\sigma^{2}}}\right\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\{\Delta^{\prime}+\Delta_{\textsc{out}}+2(\Delta^{\prime})^{1/2}\Delta_{\textsc{out}}^{1/2}\}^{1/2}\cdot\{\Delta^{\prime\prime}+\sigma^{2}\}^{1/2}

where the last line holds with probability
1-\epsilon^{\prime}
appealing to Lemmas
D.2
and
D.3
as well as the analysis for
\textsc{bias}_{1}
. In summary,

\displaystyle 2\mathbb{E}_{n}\{(\hat{\psi}_{i}-\psi_{i})\psi_{i}\}

\displaystyle\leq 2\{\Delta^{\prime}+\Delta_{\textsc{out}}+2(\Delta^{\prime})^{1/2}\Delta_{\textsc{out}}^{1/2}\}^{1/2}\cdot\{\Delta^{\prime\prime}+\sigma^{2}\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\{(\Delta^{\prime})^{1/2}+\Delta_{\textsc{out}}^{1/2}+2^{1/2}(\Delta^{\prime})^{1/4}\Delta_{\textsc{out}}^{1/4}\}\cdot\{(\Delta^{\prime\prime})^{1/2}+\sigma\}.

Taking
\textsc{bias}_{2}=2\Delta_{\textsc{out}}^{1/2}\sigma
, we have shown

2\mathbb{E}_{n}\{(\hat{\psi}_{i}-\psi_{i})\psi_{i}\}-\textsc{bias}_{2}\leq 2(\Delta^{\prime})^{1/2}\{(\Delta^{\prime\prime})^{1/2}+\sigma\}+2\Delta_{\textsc{out}}^{1/2}(\Delta^{\prime\prime})^{1/2}+2^{3/2}(\Delta^{\prime})^{1/4}\Delta_{\textsc{out}}^{1/4}\{(\Delta^{\prime\prime})^{1/2}+\sigma\}.

Thus with probability
1-\epsilon^{\prime}
,
\hat{\sigma}^{2}-(\sigma^{2}+\textsc{bias})\leq\{\hat{\sigma}^{2}-\mathbb{E}_{n}(\psi_{i}^{2})-\textsc{bias}\}+\Delta^{\prime\prime}
, equalling

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{n}\{(\hat{\psi}_{i}-\psi_{i})^{2}\}-\textsc{bias}_{1}+2\mathbb{E}_{n}\{(\hat{\psi}_{i}-\psi_{i})\psi_{i}\}-\textsc{bias}_{2}+\Delta^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\Delta^{\prime}+2(\Delta^{\prime})^{1/2}\Delta_{\textsc{out}}^{1/2}+2(\Delta^{\prime})^{1/2}\{(\Delta^{\prime\prime})^{1/2}+\sigma\}+2\Delta_{\textsc{out}}^{1/2}(\Delta^{\prime\prime})^{1/2}+2^{3/2}(\Delta^{\prime})^{1/4}\Delta_{\textsc{out}}^{1/4}\{(\Delta^{\prime\prime})^{1/2}+\sigma\}+\Delta^{\prime\prime}.

Combining terms yields the desired result.
∎

Appendix E
Additional examples

Semiparametric estimands
.
We consider causal parameters of the form

\theta_{0}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\theta_{i}
, where
\theta_{i}=\mathbb{E}[m(W_{i,\cdot},\gamma_{0})]
,
in an i.n.i.d. data generating process where

Y_{i}=\gamma_{0}(D_{i},X_{i,\cdot})+\varepsilon_{i}
,
Z_{i,\cdot}=(X_{i,\cdot}+H_{i,\cdot})\odot\pi_{i,\cdot}
, and
W_{i,\cdot}=(D_{i},X_{i,\cdot},H_{i,\cdot},\pi_{i,\cdot}).

(D_{i},X_{i,\cdot})
concatenate the various arguments of
\gamma_{0}
, which we hereby call regressors. This model includes the scenario in which some variables are corrupted and other are not.
Which regressors are corrupted or uncorrupted constrains the construction of technical regressors; see Appendix
F
. We concatentate signal and noise as
W_{i,\cdot}
. Appendix
J
generalizes Assumption
5.9
to impose invariance of the regression
\gamma_{0}
and generalized balancing weight
\alpha_{0}
across observations.

Example E.1

(Average treatment effect)
.

Let
(D_{i},X_{i,\cdot})
concatenate treatment
D_{i}\in\{0,1\}
and covariates
X_{i,\cdot}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
. Denote
\gamma_{0}(D_{i},X_{i,\cdot}):=\mathbb{E}[Y_{i}|D_{i},X_{i,\cdot}]
. Under the assumption of selection on
X_{i,\cdot}
, the average treatment effect is given by

\theta_{i}=\mathbb{E}[\gamma_{0}(1,X_{i,\cdot})-\gamma_{0}(0,X_{i,\cdot})].

With uncorrupted treatment and corrupted covariates,
W_{i,\cdot}=(D_{i},X_{i,\cdot},H_{i,\cdot},\pi_{i,\cdot})
where
(H_{i,\cdot},\pi_{i,\cdot})
are measurement error and missingness for the covariates.
9
9

9

More generally, treatment observations may be corrupted as well. For readability, we exposit the simpler and plausible case that treatment is uncorrupted.

While the true regression
\gamma_{0}(D_{i},X_{i,\cdot})
is only a function of signal
(D_{i},X_{i,\cdot})
, our regression estimator
\hat{\gamma}(D_{i},Z_{i,\cdot})
is a function of both signal and noise
W_{i,\cdot}
. In other words, the hypothesis space for estimation is the extended space of functions
\mathbb{L}_{2}(\mathcal{W})
, and we must define an extended functional over
\mathbb{L}_{2}(\mathcal{W})
. In Example
E.1
, the extended functional is

\gamma\mapsto\mathbb{E}[\gamma(1,X_{i,\cdot},H_{i,\cdot},\pi_{i,\cdot})-\gamma(0,X_{i,\cdot},H_{i,\cdot},\pi_{i,\cdot})].

Example E.2

(Local average treatment effect)
.

Let
(U_{i},X_{i,\cdot})
concatenate instrument
U_{i}\in\{0,1\}
and covariates
X_{i,\cdot}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
. Denote
\gamma_{0}(U_{i},X_{i,\cdot}):=\mathbb{E}[Y_{i}|U_{i},X_{i,\cdot}]
and
\delta_{0}(U_{i},X_{i,\cdot}):=\mathbb{E}[D_{i}|U_{i},X_{i,\cdot}]
. Under standard instrumental variable assumptions, the local average treatment effect for the subpopulation of compliers is given by

\beta_{0}=\frac{\theta_{0}}{\theta^{\prime}_{0}}
where
\theta_{i}=\mathbb{E}[\gamma_{0}(1,X_{i,\cdot})-\gamma_{0}(0,X_{i,\cdot})]
and
\theta_{i}^{\prime}=\mathbb{E}[\delta_{0}(1,X_{i,\cdot})-\delta_{0}(0,X_{i,\cdot})].

With uncorrupted instrument and corrupted covariates,
W_{i,\cdot}=(U_{i},X_{i,\cdot},H_{i,\cdot},\pi_{i,\cdot})
where
(H_{i,\cdot},\pi_{i,\cdot})
are measurement error and missingness for the covariates.

Example E.3

(Average policy effect)
.

Let
X_{i,\cdot}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
be the covariates. Consider the counterfactual transportation of covariates
x_{i,\cdot}\mapsto t(x_{i,\cdot})
. Denote
\gamma_{0}(X_{i,\cdot}):=\mathbb{E}[Y_{i}|X_{i,\cdot}]
. The average policy effect is

\theta_{i}=\mathbb{E}[\gamma_{0}\{t(X_{i,\cdot})\}-\gamma_{0}(X_{i,\cdot})].

With corrupted covariates,
W_{i,\cdot}=(X_{i,\cdot},H_{i,\cdot},\pi_{i,\cdot})
where
(H_{i,\cdot},\pi_{i,\cdot})
are measurement error and missingness for covariates.

Example E.4

(Price elasticity of demand)
.

Let
Y_{i}
be price of a particular good. Let
(D_{i},X_{i,\cdot})
concatenate quantities sold of the particular good
D_{i}
and other goods
X_{i,\cdot}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
. Denote
\gamma_{0}(D_{i},X_{i,\cdot})=\mathbb{E}[Y_{i}|D_{i},X_{i,\cdot}]
. The average price elasticity of demand is

\theta_{i}=\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\nabla_{d}\gamma_{0}(D_{i},X_{i,\cdot})}}\right].

With uncorrupted quantity for the particular good and corrupted quantities for the other goods,
W_{i,\cdot}=(D_{i},X_{i,\cdot},H_{i,\cdot},\pi_{i,\cdot})
where
(H_{i,\cdot},\pi_{i,\cdot})
are measurement error and missingness for the other goods.

Weighted estimands
. In empirical economic research with aggregate units, it is common to weight units by their size. It is also common to consider partially linear models. For example, the estimand of
[
Autor et al., 2013
]
may be viewed as a weighted partially linear instrumental variable regression. To bridge theory with practice, we provide these examples next. A weighted functional
\theta_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
is a scalar that takes the form

\theta_{0}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\theta_{i}
where
\theta_{i}=\mathbb{E}[\ell_{i}m(W_{i,\cdot},\gamma_{0})]

and
\ell_{i}
is the weight for aggregate unit
i
. For simplicity, we take the weights
\ell_{i}
to be known, but their uncertainty can be incorporated as well.

Example E.5

(Weighted partially linear regression)
.

Let
(D_{i},X_{i})
concatenate treatment
D\in\mathbb{R}
and covariates
X_{i,\cdot}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
. Denote
\gamma_{0}(D_{i},X_{i,\cdot})=\mathbb{E}[Y_{i}|D_{i},X_{i,\cdot}]
. The weighted partially regression coefficient is given by

\theta_{i}=\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\ell_{i}\{\gamma_{0}(d+1,X_{i,\cdot})-\gamma_{0}(d,X_{i,\cdot})\}}}\right].

With uncorrupted treatment and corrupted covariates,
W_{i,\cdot}=(D_{i},X_{i,\cdot},H_{i,\cdot},\pi_{i,\cdot})
where
(H_{i,\cdot},\pi_{i,\cdot})
are measurement error and missingness for the covariates.

Example E.6

(Weighted partially linear instrumental variable regression)
.

Let
(U_{i},X_{i,\cdot})
concatenate instrument
U_{i}\in\mathbb{R}
and covariates
X_{i,\cdot}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
. Denote
\gamma_{0}(U_{i},X_{i,\cdot}):=\mathbb{E}[Y_{i}|U_{i},X_{i,\cdot}]
and
\delta_{0}(U_{i},X_{i,\cdot}):=\mathbb{E}[D_{i}|U_{i},X_{i,\cdot}]
. Under standard instrumental variable assumptions, the weighted partially linear instrumental variable regression coefficient is given by

\beta_{0}=\frac{\theta_{0}}{\theta^{\prime}_{0}}
, where
\theta_{i}=\mathbb{E}[\ell_{i}\{\{\gamma_{0}(u+1,X_{i,\cdot})-\gamma_{0}(u,X_{i,\cdot})\}]
and
\theta_{i}^{\prime}=\mathbb{E}[\ell_{i}\{\delta_{0}(u+1,X_{i,\cdot})-\delta_{0}(u,X_{i,\cdot})\}].

With uncorrupted instrument and corrupted covariates,
W_{i,\cdot}=(U_{i},X_{i,\cdot},H_{i,\cdot},\pi_{i,\cdot})
where
(H_{i,\cdot},\pi_{i,\cdot})
are measurement error and missingness for the covariates.

Nonparametric estimands
. A local functional
\theta_{0}^{\lim}\in\mathbb{R}
is a scalar that takes the form

\theta^{\lim}_{0}=\lim_{h\rightarrow 0}\theta_{0}^{h}
, where
\theta_{0}^{h}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\theta^{h}_{i}
,
\theta_{i}^{h}=\mathbb{E}[m_{h}(W_{i,\cdot},\gamma_{0})]=\mathbb{E}[\ell_{h}(W_{ij})m(W_{i,\cdot},\gamma_{0})]
. Here,
\ell_{h}
is a Nadaraya Watson weighting with bandwidth
h
and
W_{ij}
is a scalar component of
W_{i,\cdot}
.
\theta^{\lim}_{0}
is a nonparametric quantity. However, it can be approximated by the sequence
\{\theta_{0}^{h}\}
. Each
\theta_{0}^{h}
can be analyzed like a weighted functional as long as we keep track of how certain quantities depend on
h
. By this logic, finite sample semiparametric theory for
\theta^{h}_{0}
translates to finite sample nonparametric theory for
\theta_{0}^{\lim}
up to some approximation error. In this sense, our analysis encompasses both semiparametric and nonparametric estimands.

Example E.7

(Heterogeneous treatment effect)
.

Let
(D_{i},V_{i},X_{i,\cdot})
concatenate treatment
D_{i}\in\{0,1\}
, covariate of interest
V_{i}\in\mathbb{R}
, and other covariates
X_{i,\cdot}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
. Denote
\gamma_{0}(D_{i},V_{i},X_{i,\cdot}):=\mathbb{E}[Y_{i}|D_{i},V_{i},X_{i,\cdot}]
. Under the assumption of selection on
(V_{i},X_{i,\cdot})
and identicial distribution of
V_{i}
, the heterogeneous treatment effect for the subpopulation with subcovariate value
v
is given by

\theta_{i}=\mathbb{E}[\gamma_{0}(1,V_{i},X_{i,\cdot})-\gamma_{0}(0,V_{i},X_{i,\cdot})|V_{i}=v]=\lim_{h\rightarrow 0}\mathbb{E}[\ell_{h}(V_{i})\{\gamma_{0}(1,V_{i},X_{i,\cdot})-\gamma_{0}(0,V_{i},X_{i,\cdot})\}]

where

\ell_{h}(V_{i})=\frac{K\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{(V_{i}-v)/h}}\right\}}{\omega}
,
\omega=\mathbb{E}[K\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{(V_{i}-v)/h}}\right\}]
,
and
K
is a standard kernel function. With uncorrupted treatment, uncorrupted covariate of interest, and corrupted other covariates,
W_{i,\cdot}=(D_{i},V_{i},X_{i,\cdot},H_{i,\cdot},\pi_{i,\cdot})
where
(H_{i,\cdot},\pi_{i,\cdot})
are measurement error and missingness for the other covariates.

Appendix
J
formally defines our general class of semiparametric and nonparametric estimands. Each example belongs to the class under generalizations of Assumption
5.10
.

Missing outcomes
. So far,
Y_{i}
has been uncorrupted. Measurement error and differential privacy of
Y_{i}
are allowed by response noise
\varepsilon_{i}
. An important additional issue is outcome attrition: for some observations,
Y_{i}
is missing in a way that may depend on the true regressors. The enriched observation model is

Y_{i}=\gamma_{0}(D_{i},X_{i,\cdot},S_{i})+\varepsilon_{i}
,

Z_{i,\cdot}=[X_{i,\cdot}+H_{i,\cdot}]\odot\pi_{i,\cdot}
, and
\tilde{Y}_{i}=Y_{i}\cdot S_{i}
with
S_{i}\in\{1,\texttt{NA}\}
. Instead of
(Y_{i},D_{i},X_{i,\cdot})
, the analyst observes
(\tilde{Y}_{i},D_{i},Z_{i,\cdot})
. Outcome
Y_{i}
may be missing at random conditional on true regressors
(D_{i},X_{i,\cdot})
, of which
X_{i,\cdot}
may be corrupted. The extended semiparametric model is

\mathbb{E}[\tilde{Y}_{i}|D_{i},X_{i,\cdot},H_{i,\cdot},\pi_{i,\cdot},S_{i}=1]=\mathbb{E}[Y_{i}|D_{i},X_{i,\cdot},H_{i,\cdot},\pi_{i,\cdot},S_{i}=1]=\mathbb{E}[Y_{i}|D_{i},X_{i,\cdot},S_{i}=1]=\gamma_{0}(D_{i},X_{i,\cdot},S_{i}=1).

For this extension, replace
Y_{i}
with
\tilde{Y}_{i}
and replace
(D,X_{i,\cdot})
with
(D_{i},X_{i,\cdot},S_{i})
.

Appendix F
Nonlinearity

We characterize the class of nonlinear dictionaries
b:\mathbb{R}^{p}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{p^{\prime}}
for which our main results go through. We discuss two classes of dictionaries and delay proofs to the end.

Polynomial dictionary
. We refer to the following three simple properties as dictionary continuity, since they imply that the data cleaning results for original regressors imply similar data cleaning results for technical regressors constructed from the dictionary. We state the properties then verify them for the polynomial dictionary of degree
d_{\max}
.

Assumption F.1

(Dictionary continuity)
.

(i) For any two matrices
\boldsymbol{M}^{(1)},\boldsymbol{M}^{(2)}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times p}
,

\|b(\boldsymbol{M}^{(1)})-b(\boldsymbol{M}^{(2)})\|^{2}_{2,\infty}\leq C_{b}^{\prime}\|\boldsymbol{M}^{(1)}-\boldsymbol{M}^{(2)}\|^{2}_{2,\infty}
;
(ii) for any
\boldsymbol{M}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times p}
,
rank\{b(\boldsymbol{M})\}\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{rank(\boldsymbol{M})}}\right\}^{C_{b}^{\prime\prime}}
;
(iii) for any
v\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
,
\|b(v)\|_{\max}\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\|v\|_{\max}}}\right)^{C_{b}^{\prime\prime\prime}}
.

For much of our argument to go through, it suffices that the dictionary exhibits three simple properties: clean original regressors imply clean technical regressors; low rank original regressors imply low rank technical regressors; and bounded original regressors imply bounded technical regressors. Polynomial dictionaries have these properties.

Definition F.1

(Polynomial dictionary)
.

Let
v=(v_{1},v_{2},\dots,v_{p})\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
.
Consider the dictionary
b^{\textsc{poly}}
, where for
k\in[p^{\prime}]
,
b^{\textsc{poly}}_{k}(v)=\prod_{\ell=1}^{d(k)}v_{\ell}
with
v_{\ell}\in\{v_{1},\dots,v_{p}\}
.

That is, each basis function
b^{\textsc{poly}}_{k}(v)
in the dictionary is a polynomial of degree
d(k)\leq d_{\max}
constructed from coordinates of
v
, allowing for repeats. This class of dictionaries is commonly used in empirical economic research. It nests as a special case the interacted dictionary studied in the main text, which permits a rich model of heterogeneous treatment effects. Pleasingly, for this class, the dictionary constants
(C_{b}^{\prime},C_{b}^{\prime\prime},C_{b}^{\prime\prime\prime})
do not depend on
p^{\prime}
. Rather,
(C_{b}^{\prime},C_{b}^{\prime\prime},C_{b}^{\prime\prime\prime})
depend on the maximum degree
d_{\max}
of the polynomial dictionary.

Proposition F.1

(Verifying dictionary continuity)
.

b^{\textsc{poly}}
of degree
d_{\max}
satisfies Assumption
F.1
with
C_{b}^{\prime}\leq 2^{d_{\max}}\cdot\|\boldsymbol{M}^{(1)}\|^{2d_{\max}}_{\max}\cdot\|\boldsymbol{M}^{(2)}\|^{2d_{\max}}_{\max}
,

C_{b}^{\prime\prime}\leq d_{\max}
, and
C_{b}^{\prime\prime\prime}\leq d_{\max}
.

This class of dictionaries preserves the low rank approximation in the following sense.

Proposition F.2

(Low rank approximation is preserved)
.

Suppose Assumption
5.1
holds and the true covariates satisfy
\boldsymbol{X}=\boldsymbol{X}^{\textsc{(lr)}}+\boldsymbol{E}^{\textsc{(lr)}}
where
r=rank\{\boldsymbol{X}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\}
and
\Delta_{E}=\|\boldsymbol{E}^{\textsc{(lr)}}\|_{\max}
. Consider
b=b^{\textsc{poly}}
of degree
d_{\max}
. Then

r^{\prime}:=rank\{b(\boldsymbol{X}^{\textsc{(lr)}})\}\leq r^{d_{\max}}
and
\Delta^{\prime}_{E}:=\|b(\boldsymbol{X})-b(\boldsymbol{X}^{\textsc{(lr)}})\|_{\max}\leq C\bar{A}^{d_{\max}}\cdot d_{\max}\Delta_{E}
.

The same logic applies for dictionaries applied to
(D_{i},X_{i,\cdot})
rather than
X_{i,\cdot}
. The generalization of Appendix
A
with nonlinear dictionaries is immediate from these results.

Polynomial dictionary with uncorrupted nonlinearity
.
Assumption
F.1
suffices to generalize our data cleaning results. For analysis of the error-in-variable estimators, we impose a further assumption, which constrains which kinds of terms can appear as technical regressors. Consider the polynomial dictionary of degree
d_{\max}
, where the only source of nonlinearity is powers and interactions with regressors known to be uncorrupted.

Definition F.2

(Polynomial dictionary with uncorrupted nonlinearity)
.

Suppose the observed regressors consist of one uncorrupted regressor
D_{i}
and several corrupted regressors
X_{i,\cdot}
. Consider a polynomial dictionary
b^{\textsc{poly}}
of degree
d_{\max}
such that each basis function
b^{\textsc{poly}}_{k}
is at most linear in the corrupted regressors. By definition,
p^{\prime}\leq C\cdot d_{\max}p
.

For example, in Example
E.1
where
D_{i}
is uncorrupted, the interacted dictionary

b:(D_{i},X_{i,\cdot})\mapsto\{D_{i}X_{i,\cdot},(1-D_{i})X_{i,\cdot}\}

satisfies this property.
In Example
E.4
where
D_{i}
is uncorrupted, the nonlinear dictionary

b:(D_{i},X_{i,\cdot})\mapsto(1,D_{i},X_{i,\cdot},D_{i}X_{i,\cdot},D_{i}^{2})
satisfies this property as well since it contains
D_{i}^{2}
but does not contain
X_{ij}^{2}
.
